    \newsavebox\pandoc@box
    \newcommand*\pandocbounded[1]{%
      \sbox\pandoc@box{#1}%
      % scaling factors for width and height
      \Gscale@div\@tempa\textheight{\dimexpr\ht\pandoc@box+\dp\pandoc@box\relax}%
      \Gscale@div\@tempb\linewidth{\wd\pandoc@box}%
      % select the smaller of both
      \ifdim\@tempb\p@<\@tempa\p@
        \let\@tempa\@tempb
      \fi
      % scaling accordingly (\@tempa < 1)
      \ifdim\@tempa\p@<\p@
        \scalebox{\@tempa}{\usebox\pandoc@box}%
      % scaling not needed, use as it is
      \else
        \usebox{\pandoc@box}%
      \fi
    }
    \makeatother

\def\PY@reset{\let\PY@it=\relax \let\PY@bf=\relax%
    \let\PY@ul=\relax \let\PY@tc=\relax%
    \let\PY@bc=\relax \let\PY@ff=\relax}
\def\PY@tok#1{\csname PY@tok@#1\endcsname}
\def\PY@toks#1+{\ifx\relax#1\empty\else%
    \PY@tok{#1}\expandafter\PY@toks\fi}
\def\PY@do#1{\PY@bc{\PY@tc{\PY@ul{%
    \PY@it{\PY@bf{\PY@ff{#1}}}}}}}
\def\PY#1#2{\PY@reset\PY@toks#1+\relax+\PY@do{#2}}

    % For linebreaks inside Verbatim environment from package fancyvrb.
    \makeatletter
        \newbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox
        \newbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox
        \newcommand*\Wrappedvisiblespace {\textcolor{red}{\textvisiblespace}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol {\textcolor{red}{\llap{\tiny$\m@th\hookrightarrow$}}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationindent {3ex }
        \newcommand*\Wrappedafterbreak {\kern\Wrappedcontinuationindent\copy\Wrappedcontinuationbox}
        % Take advantage of the already applied Pygments mark-up to insert
        % potential linebreaks for TeX processing.
        %        {, <, #, %, $, ' and ": go to next line.
        %        _, }, ^, &, >, - and ~: stay at end of broken line.
        % Use of \textquotesingle for straight quote.
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatspecials {%
            \def\PYGZus{\discretionary{\char`\_}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\_}}%
            \def\PYGZob{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\{}{\char`\{}}%
            \def\PYGZcb{\discretionary{\char`\}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\}}}%
            \def\PYGZca{\discretionary{\char`\^}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\^}}%
            \def\PYGZam{\discretionary{\char`\&}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\&}}%
            \def\PYGZlt{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\<}{\char`\<}}%
            \def\PYGZgt{\discretionary{\char`\>}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\>}}%
            \def\PYGZsh{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\#}{\char`\#}}%
            \def\PYGZpc{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\%}{\char`\%}}%
            \def\PYGZdl{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\$}{\char`\$}}%
            \def\PYGZhy{\discretionary{\char`\-}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\-}}%
            \def\PYGZsq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\textquotesingle}{\textquotesingle}}%
            \def\PYGZdq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\"}{\char`\"}}%
            \def\PYGZti{\discretionary{\char`\~}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\~}}%
        }
        % Some characters . , ; ? ! / are not pygmentized.
        % This macro makes them "active" and they will insert potential linebreaks
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatpunct {%
            \lccode`\~`\.\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\.}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\.}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\,\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\,}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\,}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\;\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\;}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\;}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\:\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\:}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\:}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\?\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\?}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\?}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\!\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\!}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\!}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\/\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\/}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\/}}}%
            \catcode`\.\active
            \catcode`\,\active
            \catcode`\;\active
            \catcode`\:\active
            \catcode`\?\active
            \catcode`\!\active
            \catcode`\/\active
            \lccode`\~`\~
        }
    \makeatother

    \let\OriginalVerbatim=\Verbatim
    \makeatletter
    \renewcommand{\Verbatim}[1][1]{%
        %\parskip\z@skip
        \sbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox {\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol}%
        \sbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox {\FV@SetupFont\Wrappedvisiblespace}%
        \def\FancyVerbFormatLine ##1{\hsize\linewidth
            \vtop{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty\z@\exhyphenpenalty\z@
                \doublehyphendemerits\z@\finalhyphendemerits\z@
                \strut ##1\strut}%
        }%
        % If the linebreak is at a space, the latter will be displayed as visible
        % space at end of first line, and a continuation symbol starts next line.
        % Stretch/shrink are however usually zero for typewriter font.
        \def\FV@Space {%
            \nobreak\hskip\z@ plus\fontdimen3\font minus\fontdimen4\font
            \discretionary{\copy\Wrappedvisiblespacebox}{\Wrappedafterbreak}
            {\kern\fontdimen2\font}%
        }%

        % Allow breaks at special characters using \PYG... macros.
        \Wrappedbreaksatspecials
        % Breaks at punctuation characters . , ; ? ! and / need catcode=\active
        \OriginalVerbatim[#1,codes*=\Wrappedbreaksatpunct]%
    }
    \makeatother

    \section{Introduction}\label{introduction}

Before exploring what depth of field is and how it can be controlled, I
want to first take a step back and explain some basics about camera
lenses. In particular, I'll be covering Focal Length and Aperture.

    \subsubsection{Focal Length}\label{focal-length}

\begin{quote}
``Focal length, usually represented in millimeters (mm), is the basic
description of a photographic lens. It is not a measurement of the
actual length of a lens, but a calculation of an optical distance from
the point where light rays converge to form a sharp image of an object
to the digital sensor or 35mm film at the focal plane in a camera. The
focal length of a lens is determined when the lens is focused at
infinity.''
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
``Lens focal length tells us the angle of view--how much of the scene
will be captured--and the magnification--how large the individual
elements will be. The longer the focal length, the narrower the angle of
view and the higher the magnification. The shorter the focal length, the
wider the angle of view and the lower the magnification.''
\end{quote}

As an example, let's look at three typical focal lengths and see how
their different angles of view can alter the way a scene is captured.
Below are three photos of the same object from roughly the same distance
that I've taken with 28mm, 50mm, and 105mm lenses.

    Focal Length comparison between 28mm, 50mm, and 105mm lenses

    \subsubsection{Aperture}\label{aperture}

A camera lens aperture is the opening within a lens that controls the
amount of light which can pass through it. Typically a diaphragm is used
to reduce or enlarge the effective diameter of the lens opening in order
to decrease or increase the amount of light that is exposed onto the
camera sensor. Lens aperture is usually measured in ``F-Stops'' which
are determined by the ratio of the focal length to the effective
aperture diameter where a lower f-stop refers to a larger lens opening
while a higher f-stop refers to a smaller one.

The maximum and minimum aperture sizes are normally displayed on the
outside of a lens and one could expect to see a typical range of
f/2.8-f/16 on an average lens with ``faster'' (larger opening) lenses
capable of f/2, f/1.4, or even f/0.95. Below are some photos I took of a
50mm lens with the aperture set at each stop between f/1.4 and f/16.

    50mm Lens with Aperture set to every full stop between f/1.4 and f/16

    \section{Depth of Field}\label{depth-of-field}

\begin{quote}
``Depth of Field (DOF) is the distance between the nearest and the
farthest objects that are in acceptably sharp focus in an image captured
with a camera.''
\end{quote}

\[
    DOF \approx \frac{2u^2Nc}{f^2}
\]

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  f = Lens Focal Length (mm)
\item
  N = f-number
\item
  u = Distance to Subject (mm)
\item
  c = Circle of Confusion (mm)
\end{itemize}

To visualize the relationship between depth of field and subject
distance, I calculated the approximate depth of field for three lenses
with a constant aperture (f/4) and a focus range of 0.5 to 4m.

        \PY{n}{ticks}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{outside}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{showgrid}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{gridcolor}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{lightgrey}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{yaxis}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n+nb}{dict}\PY{p}{(}

            \PY{n}{text}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZlt{}b\PYZgt{}Depth of Field (m)\PYZlt{}/b\PYZgt{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{rangemode}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{tozero}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} dtick=1000,}

\end{tcolorbox}

    
    
    

    Here you can see that as subject distance increases, so does depth of
field. This is especially true as you decrease lens focal length.

This leads to an interesting question, at what distance should I focus
the lens if I want everything in the background to be in focus?

    \subsection{Hyperfocal Distance}\label{hyperfocal-distance}

\begin{quote}
``Hyperfocal distance is the closest distance at which a lens can be
focused while keeping objects at infinity acceptably sharp. When the
lens is focused at this distance, all objects at distances from half of
the hyperfocal distance out to infinity will be acceptably sharp.''
\end{quote}

\[
    H = \frac{f^2}{Nc}+f
\]

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  H = Hyperfocal Distance (mm)
\item
  f = Lens Focal Length (mm)
\item
  N = f-number
\item
  c = Circle of Confusion (mm)
\end{itemize}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{38}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} example hyperfocal distance calculation}
\PY{n}{f} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{50}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{N} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{8}
\PY{n}{c} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.025}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}

    \subsection{Near Focus Limit \& Far Focus
Limit}\label{near-focus-limit-far-focus-limit}

\begin{quote}
``The Near Focus Limit (NF) is the distance from where acceptable focus
starts when you focus at a given distance.''
\end{quote}

\[
    NF = \frac{Hu}{H+(u-f)}
\]

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  NF = Near Focus Limit (mm)
\item
  H = Hyperfocal Distance (mm)
\item
  u = Distance to Subject (mm)
\item
  f = Lens Focal Length (mm)
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
``The Far Focus Limit (FF) is the distance where acceptable focus ends
when you focus at a given distance.''
\end{quote}

\[
    FF = \frac{Hu}{H-(u-f)}
\]

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  FF = Far Focus Limit (mm)
\item
  H = Hyperfocal Distance (mm)
\item
  u = Distance to Subject (mm)
\item
  f = Lens Focal Length (mm)
\end{itemize}

In addition to the DOF approximation formula discussed earlier, Depth of
Field can be also be calculated by subtracting the Near Focus Limit from
the Far Focus Limit. \[
    DOF = FF-NF
\]

    \subsection{Subject Distance vs.~Depth of
Field}\label{subject-distance-vs.-depth-of-field}

To create a more realistic visualization of how this applies to everyday
photography, I calculated the near and far focus limits of a 35mm lens
with aperture set to f/4 at focus distances of 1, 2, and 3 meters away.
Below is a diagram that I made using the results of my calculations.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{39}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} SUBJECT DISTANCE VS. DEPTH OF FIELD (35mm focal length)}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} calculate the near focus limit and far focus limit at 3 different focus distances}
\PY{n}{f} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{35}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{N} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}
\PY{n}{c} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.025}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{u} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}

\PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1000}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{2000}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3000}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}aov} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{diagonal\PYZus{}angle\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}view\PYZus{}deg}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{focal\PYZus{}length\PYZus{}mm}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{values}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateHyperFocalDistance}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}
    \PY{n}{nf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{+} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{nf\PYZus{}mm}

\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}a\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}b\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}c\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}

    For reference, I made an alternate version of the Subject Distance
vs.~Depth of Field diagram using a 105mm lens to highlight the impact of
using telephoto lenses when looking to capture a narrow depth of field.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} SUBJECT DISTANCE VS. DEPTH OF FIELD (105mm focal length)}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} calculate the near focus limit and far focus limit at 3 different focus distances}
\PY{n}{f} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{105}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{N} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}
\PY{n}{c} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.025}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{u} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}

\PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1000}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{2000}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3000}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}aov} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{diagonal\PYZus{}angle\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}view\PYZus{}deg}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{focal\PYZus{}length\PYZus{}mm}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{values}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateHyperFocalDistance}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}
    \PY{n}{nf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{+} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{nf\PYZus{}mm}

\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}a\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}b\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}c\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{focus\PYZus{}distances}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{calculateFarFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}

    \PY{n}{ff\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}

    105mm - f/4 - 1/60

    \subsection{Aperture vs.~Depth of
Field}\label{aperture-vs.-depth-of-field}

    Besides controlling the amount of light that reaches the camera sensor,
aperture also can increase or decrease the overall depth of field. In
the following diagram I calculated and plotted the depth of field of a
35mm lens focused to 2m with aperture set to f/1.4, f/4, and f/11.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{41}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} APERTURE VS. DEPTH OF FIELD}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} calculate the near focus limit and far focus limit at 3 different aperture settings}
\PY{n}{f} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{35}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{N} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}
\PY{n}{c} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.025}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}
\PY{n}{u} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{2000}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} mm}

\PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mf}{1.4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{11}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}aov} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{diagonal\PYZus{}angle\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}view\PYZus{}deg}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{loc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{lens\PYZus{}df}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{focal\PYZus{}length\PYZus{}mm}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{values}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateHyperFocalDistance}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}
    \PY{n}{nf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{+} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{nf\PYZus{}mm}

\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}a\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}b\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}near\PYZus{}c\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateNearFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{calculateFarFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateHyperFocalDistance}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}stops}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}
    \PY{n}{ff\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{hf\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{u\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{f\PYZus{}mm}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{ff\PYZus{}mm}

\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}far\PYZus{}a\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateFarFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}far\PYZus{}b\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateFarFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{dof\PYZus{}far\PYZus{}c\PYZus{}mm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{calculateFarFocusLimit}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{aperture\PYZus{}settings}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{u}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsection{Focal Length vs.~Depth of
Field}\label{focal-length-vs.-depth-of-field}

To bring everything back to where I began this study, let's take a look
at the impact of varying focal lengths on depth of field using a
constant aperture and focus distance.
